24 of the Inams Abolition Act and remanded the matter for

,, '', I 10. Upon careful consideration of the pleadings, rival
1.*
' , ,r, submissions and the material placed on record, this Court
. finds that the controversy does not warrant adjudication on
merits of rival title or entitlement at this stage, but turns
squarely on procedural legality, jurisdictional limits and
obseruance of principles of natural justice in the appellate
proceedings culminating in the impugned order dated
22.07.2024.
11. It is not in dispute that subsequent to the grant of
Occupancy Rights Certificates (ORCs) by the Revenue
Divisional Officer on 05. 10.2023/07.LO.2A23, the petitioners
purchased the subject lands under registered sale deeds for
valid consideration and that their names were mutated and
reflected as pattadars in the statutory Record of Rights
maintained on the Dharani/Bhu Bharathi portals. The appeals

Page l8 of25
before the Additional Collector were instituted thereafter and
admittedly with'knowledge of the alienations.
12. The Appellate Authority, while exercising
jurisdictiorr under Section 24 of the Telangana Abolition of
Inams Act, 1955, failed to implead the petitioners, who were
admittedly persons having subsisting civil and statutory
interests in the subject lands and proceeded to pass orders
adversely affecting their rights. The declaration that the
registered sale deeds, pattadar passbooks and mutations in
favour of the petitioners are "null and void" was made
without iss;uance of notice or affording an opportunity of
hearing. in patent violation of the principles of audi alteram
partem. Any order having civil consequences passed behind
the back of affected parties is vitiated in law.
13. Further, this Court finds considerable force in the
contention that the Appellate Authority travelled beyond the
scope of Serction 24 of the Inams Abolition Act. The appellate
power thereunder is confined to examining the legality or

Page 19 of25
propriety of the order granting or refusing occupancy rights.
It does not extend to annulling registered conveyances or
- extinguishing statutory entries made under the Registration
Act, the Telangana Rights in Land and Pattadar Pass Books
Act.,2O2O, or the Transfer of Property Act. Such declarations
fall exclusively within the domain of competent civil Courts.
To that extent, the impugned order suffers from jurisdictional
overreach.
L4. At the same time, this Court is unable to accede
to the petitioners' prayer for restoration of the ORCs as a fait
accompli. The record discloses serious and substantial
allegations raised by the appellants before the Appellate
Authority touching upon non-issuance or improper seruice of
Form-II notices, non-verification of heirship, ceiling
enquiry under the Inams Abolition Act and cannot be
declarations, possession, tenancy claims and the shares of all
inamdars. These aspects go to the root of the statutory
conclusively determined in writ jurisdiction.

Page 20 of25
15. The claim of the implead petitioners was that
upon grant of ORCs-iR"faVour of their vendors and by virtue
of the subsequent purchases, valuable rights accrued to the
petitioners through conveyance under registered sale deeds
for valuable consideration. Therefore, the implead petitioners
have a subsisting and real interest in the matter to present
their cas€:, as their vendors, having already sold the
property, wouJd have lesser interest in prosecuting the
proceedings. Since it is contended that no opportunity was
afforded to the petitioners to demonstrate their case as
subsequent purchaserc after grant of ORCs in favour of their
vendors and instead of directing the appellate authority to
undertake the exercise afresh, this Court notes that the
matter is required to be decided by the primary authority.
16. tt is alleged that the RDO issued notices through
WhatsApp, which is not a mode recognized under law and
that such notices were issued in a hurried manner without
conducting the proceedinqs in accordance with law.

Page 21 of25
17. The Hon'ble Supreme Court in Satender Kumar
Antil vs. cBtr2 {IA No. 63691 of 2025 in MA No. 2O34 of
2,422 in MA No. 1849 of ZOZL in Special Leave Petition (Crl.)
No. 5191 of ZOZL, dated L6.7.2025) held that service of the
notices through WhatsApp or other modes of electronic
commun.ication, cannot be considered or recognized as an
alternative or substitute to the mode of service recognized
and prescribed under the Cr.PC.1973IBNSS, 2023.
18. The Appellate Authority was, therefore, justified in
principle in holding that the order of the RDO warranted
interference and in directing a de-novo enquiry. However,
the manner in which such power was exercised by nullifylng
third-pafty rights without notice and by recording findings
affecting title cannot be sustained.
19. In view of the above facts and circumstances,
without expressing any opinion on the merits or demerits of
2 zozs scc onLine sc 1578

with law and to adjudicate the .matter as expeditiousty as
possible
24. In that view of the matter, the interests of justice

.
to the limited extent of its jurisdictional excess and violation
